The complete retry runs all twelve planned processes: three interleaved original/candidate rounds at 14 and 22 GB ceilings. All physical envelopes and natural-completion flags pass, and every cell is eligible under the frozen prospective pilot policy. The policy allows at most one MiB of aggregate whole-host swap-ins per process; one original 22-GB process records 851,968 bytes and every other process records zero. All record zero swap-outs. This is a descriptive pilot, not the old held-out statistical qualification or a universal throughput claim.

The first v8 attempt completes three cells and then refuses the next process after persistent Photos background CPU prevents its quiet-start gate. Its complete prefix and failed coordinator remain separately preserved and are not combined with the retry. During the full retry a bounded identity-checking guard pauses only the current user's mediaanalysisd and resumes it automatically when the owned benchmark exits; its terminal receipt confirms restoration. A parent-bound, one-hour caffeinate assertion also ends. The application window is restored after collection. These conditions, the continuously quiet two-minute admissions and uncontrolled filesystem cache limit interpretation to controlled interactive bursts on the available Mac.

The frozen full analysis finds the candidate generally slower in generation, while reducing long-context first-text and full-request latency. No default promotion follows. The nested prefetch diagnostics suggest a concrete configuration lead: the original uses corrected attention forecasting, whereas the candidate uses the older boundary tap. These overlapping counters are not independent cost attribution or proof of causality. Existing attention forecasting is the next bounded engineering check; do not borrow the original correction's quality or timing qualification.

The exact-head engine CI for e0bc6433a09567e01b351b8d7028f9ebfa6d0cf8 passes every job. Raw inputs, outputs, supervision, full analysis, host guard and CI receipt follow. Each file is reconstructed exactly by zlib decompression of its base64 body and checked against its original byte count and SHA-256. No weights are embedded.
